While at the same time, while the price does this, we want the RSI to print one low
---
So, we want to see lower lows on the price and higher lows on the indicators.
---
When we're talking about bullish RSI divergence, you should only look at the lows.
---
When we're talking about bullish divergence, only focus on the lows.
---
And the same thing is true, but the opposite for the bearish divergence.
---
So, when it comes to the bearish divergence, this is when the price uh prints one high and then a higher high.
---
While the RSI prints one high and then a lower high.
---
And notice here that when we're when we're talking about the bearish divergence, we're talking about highs.
---
This is by far the most common mistake when it comes to divergences.

So, if you only take away one thing is that, you know, when what when I focus on bullish divergence, focus on lows.

When we focus on bearish divergence, focus on highs.

So, for example, if we have a 50 period moving average, and the most simple moving average is a SMA, which stands for simple moving average.
---
So, here, the volume is the trades per candle, the moving average is the average price, and the VWAP is when we combine the volume and the average.
---

A fair value gap is just a three candle pattern where the high of candle one and the low of candle three don't overlap and this leaves a sort of price gap that wasn't fully traded through.
